1	---
2	blueprint:
3	  name: "Lock Code Manager: Slot Usage Notifier"
4	  description: >
5	    Runs one or more actions when a Lock Code Manager code slot PIN
6	    is used on a lock. Use this to send notifications, trigger scripts,
7	    or run any other Home Assistant action.
8	
9	
10	    **How it works**
11	
12	    When a code slot event fires (PIN used on lock), the automation
13	    runs the configured actions. You can use the action editor to
14	    configure any HA action — notifications, scripts, scenes, etc.
15	
16	
17	    **Available template variables**
18	
19	    Your actions can reference these variables in templates:
20	
21	    - `slot_num` — the code slot number that was used
22	
23	    - `slot_name` — the user-configured name of the code slot
24	
25	    - `lock_name` — the friendly name of the lock where the PIN
26	      was used
27	
28	    - `timestamp` — the date and time the event occurred
29	
30	
31	    **Requirements**
32	
33	    This blueprint requires at least one lock that supports code slot
34	    events (i.e. produces `*_code_slot_*_pin_used` events).
35	
36	
37	    **Setup**
38	
39	    1. Import this blueprint and create an automation from it.
40	
41	    2. Select the code slot event entity to monitor.
42	
43	    3. Configure the actions to run when the PIN is used.
44	  domain: automation
45	  # yamllint disable-line rule:line-length
46	  source_url: https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/blob/main/blueprints/automation/lock_code_manager/slot_usage_notifier.yaml
47	  input:
48	    event_entity:
49	      name: Code slot event entity
50	      description: >
51	        The Lock Code Manager event entity that fires when a PIN
52	        is used. These are named like "Code slot X PIN used".
53	      selector:
54	        entity:
55	          domain: event
56	          integration: lock_code_manager
57	    notify_actions:
58	      name: Actions
59	      description: >
60	        The actions to run when a PIN is used. You can configure
61	        any HA action here — notifications, scripts, etc. Use
62	        template variables like `{{ slot_name }}` and
63	        `{{ lock_name }}` in your action data.
64	      selector:
65	        action:
66	
67	mode: queued
68	max: 10
69	
70	triggers:
71	  - trigger: state
72	    entity_id: !input event_entity
73	    not_to:
74	      - unknown
75	      - unavailable
76	
77	variables:
78	  event_entity_id: !input event_entity
79	  slot_name: >-
80	    {{ trigger.to_state.attributes.code_slot_name
81	       | default('Unknown slot') }}

83	    {{ trigger.to_state.attributes.code_slot

91

1	---
2	blueprint:
3	  name: "Lock Code Manager: Slot Usage Limiter"
4	  description: >
5	    Decrements an `input_number` helper each time a Lock Code Manager code
6	    slot PIN is used on the lock. When the counter reaches 0, the Lock Code
7	    Manager slot is automatically disabled. Optionally resets the counter
8	    when the slot is re-enabled in Lock Code Manager.
9	
10	
11	    **Special values**
12	
13	    - Set the counter to **-1** for unlimited uses (counter stays at -1,
14	      slot is never disabled).
15	
16	    - Set the counter to **0** to immediately disable the slot on next use.
17	
18	
19	    **Requirements**
20	
21	    This blueprint requires at least one lock that supports code slot events
22	    (i.e. produces `*_code_slot_*_pin_used` events). If none of your locks
23	    support this, the counter will never decrement.
24	
25	
26	    **Setup**
27	
28	    1. Create an `input_number` helper (Settings → Devices & Services → Helpers)
29	       with min=-1, max=your desired limit, and step=1.
30	
31	    2. Import this blueprint and configure it with the PIN used event entity,
32	       enabled switch entity, and the helper you just created.
33	  domain: automation
34	  # yamllint disable-line rule:line-length
35	  source_url: https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/blob/main/blueprints/automation/lock_code_manager/slot_usage_limiter.yaml
36	  input:
37	    config_entry:
38	      name: Lock Code Manager config entry
39	      description: >
40	        The Lock Code Manager config entry that manages your lock(s).
41	        Used to identify the setup in notifications.
42	      selector:
43	        config_entry:
44	          integration: lock_code_manager
45	    slot_number:
46	      name: Code slot number
47	      description: The Lock Code Manager code slot number.
48	      selector:
49	        number:
50	          min: 1
51	          max: 999
52	          mode: box
53	    pin_used_entity:
54	      name: PIN used event entity
55	      description: >
56	        The Lock Code Manager event entity that fires when the PIN is used
57	        (e.g. `event.front_door_code_slot_1_pin_used`).
58	      selector:
59	        entity:
60	          domain: event
61	          integration: lock_code_manager
62	    enabled_switch:
63	      name: Slot enabled switch
64	      description: >
65	        The Lock Code Manager switch entity that enables/disables the code
66	        slot (e.g. `switch.front_door_code_slot_1_enabled`).
67	      selector:
68	        entity:
69	          domain: switch
70	          integration: lock_code_manager
71	    uses_counter:
72	      name: Uses counter
73	      description: >
74	        An `input_number` helper that tracks remaining uses. Set to -1 for
75	        unlimited uses. Create one via Settings → Devices & Services → Helpers
76	        (set min to -1).
77	      selector:
78	        entity:
79	          domain: input_number
80	    initial_uses:
81	      name: Initial uses on re-enable
82	      description: >
83	        When greater than 0, the counter is automatically reset to this value
84	        each time the Lock Code Manager slot is re-enabled. Set to 0 to
85	        disable auto-reset. Set to -1 for unlimited uses on re-enable.
86	      default: 0
87	      selector:
88	        number:
89	          min: -1
90	          max: 999
91	          mode: box
92	    notify_target:
93	      name: Notification service (optional)
94	      description: >
95	        A notify entity to send a message to when uses are exhausted and the
96	        slot is disabled. Leave empty to skip notifications.
97	      default: ""
98	      selector:
99	        entity:
100	          domain: notify
101	
102	mode: queued
103	max: 10
104	
105	triggers:
106	  - trigger: state
107	    entity_id: !input pin_used_entity
108	    id: pin_used
109	  - trigger: state
110	    entity_id: !input enabled_switch
111	    id: slot_enabled
112	    to: "on"
113	
114	variables:
115	  initial_uses: !input initial_uses
116	  uses_counter: !input uses_counter
117	  notify_target: !input notify_target
118	  _config_entry: !input config_entry
119	  _config_entry_title: >
120	    {{ config_entry_attr(_config_entry, 'title') }}
121	  _slot_number: !input slot_number
122	
123	actions:
124	  - choose:
125	      - conditions:
126	          - condition: trigger
127	            id: pin_used
128	        sequence:
129	          - variables:
130	              current: >
131	                {{ states(uses_counter) | int(0) }}
132	          # -1 means unlimited — skip decrement and disable
133	          - if:
134	              - condition: template
135	                value_template: "{{ current == -1 }}"
136	            then:
137	              - stop: "Unlimited uses — no action needed"
138	          - variables:
139	              new_count: >
140	                {{ [current - 1, 0] | max }}
141	          - action: input_number.set_value
142	            target:
143	              entity_id: !input uses_counter
144	            data:
145	              value: "{{ new_count }}"
146	          - if:
147	              - condition: template
148	                value_template: "{{ new_count == 0 }}"
149	            then:
150	              - action: switch.turn_off
151	                target:
152	                  entity_id: !input enabled_switch
153	              - if:
154	                  - condition: template
155	                    value_template: "{{ notify_target != '' }}"
156	                then:
157	                  - action: notify.send_message
158	                    target:
159	                      entity_id: "{{ notify_target }}"
160	                    data:
161	                      title: >
162	                        {{ _config_entry_title }} — Slot {{ _slot_number }}
163	                        Usage Limit Reached
164	                      message: >
165	                        {{ _config_entry_title }} code slot {{ _slot_number }}
166	                        has been disabled after reaching the usage limit.
167	                        Last used on lock
168	                        {{ state_attr(trigger.entity_id, 'event_type')
169	                           | default('unknown') }}
170	                        at {{ now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S') }}.
171	      - conditions:
172	          - condition: trigger
173	            id: slot_enabled
174	          - condition: template
175	            value_template: "{{ initial_uses | int(0) != 0 }}"
176	        sequence:
177	          - action: input_number.set_value
178	            target:
179	              entity_id: !input uses_counter
180	            data:
181	              value: !input initial_uses
182

> AGENT

Good — I have enough context. The `event_type` attribute on the event entity holds the lock's entity ID. I'll add the input + plumbing, then hand you the filter template to write (it's the part with real design choices).

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- In this codebase's LCM event entities, `state.attributes.event_type` carries the lock's entity ID (see line 86–87 — it's used as the entity_id argument to `state_attr`). That makes it the natural join key for "which lock fired this."
- HA's `entity` selector with `multiple: true` always returns a list — never a string — so the template doesn't have to handle a scalar fallback. An unset/cleared input becomes `[]`, which is what makes the "empty list = allow all" pattern clean.
- Top-level `conditions:` is preferable to wrapping `!input notify_actions` in an `if`: the automation trace shows the skip explicitly, and the user's injected action block stays untouched.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> AGENT

Yes — one strong candidate: **`slot_usage_limiter.yaml`**. It has the same shape as the notifier:

- Triggers on a single per-slot `pin_used_entity` that fires for any lock under the LCM config (line 107 in `slot_usage_limiter.yaml`).
- The decrement runs regardless of which lock fired.
- Realistic scenario: a slot is shared across front + back door, but you only want a guest's 5-use quota to apply to the front door — uses on the back door shouldn't count.

The shape would be identical: optional multi-select `locks` input, default `[]`, gated by a condition on `trigger.to_state.attributes.event_type` inside the `pin_used` choose-branch.

The other blueprints aren't candidates:
- **`calendar_pin_setter.yaml`** — triggers on calendar/state, not on PIN events; it writes PINs rather than reacting to lock usage.
- **`condition_linker.yaml`** — triggers on a custom event from LCM internals, not lock-specific.
- **`auto_relock.yaml` / `door_lock_on_close.yaml`** — already bound to a single lock entity by design.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- The pattern only applies when a blueprint subscribes to an event entity that fires for *multiple locks under one slot*. That's exactly the LCM `event.*_code_slot_*_pin_used` shape — most other LCM-related blueprints are either pre-scoped to a single lock or aren't reacting to lock events at all.
- For the limiter, the filter has a sharper semantic than for the notifier: filtering decrements vs. filtering side-effects. Worth wording the input description carefully — e.g., "uses on excluded locks won't count toward the limit" — so users understand the quota implication.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Want me to open a follow-up PR for the limiter? Per your `feedback_worktrees_subagents.md`, I'd do it in a separate branch so the notifier PR stays focused — and could spawn a worktree if you want it in parallel rather than after #1152 merges.

1	---
2	blueprint:
3	  name: "Lock Code Manager: Slot Usage Limiter"
4	  description: >
5	    Decrements an `input_number` helper each time a Lock Code Manager code
6	    slot PIN is used on the lock. When the counter reaches 0, the Lock Code
7	    Manager slot is automatically disabled. Optionally resets the counter
8	    when the slot is re-enabled in Lock Code Manager. Optionally restricts
9	    the limit to a specific lock or set of locks.
10	
11	
12	    **Special values**
13	
14	    - Set the counter to **-1** for unlimited uses (counter stays at -1,
15	      slot is never disabled).
16	
17	    - Set the counter to **0** to immediately disable the slot on next use.
18	
19	
20	    **Requirements**
21	
22	    This blueprint requires at least one lock that supports code slot events
23	    (i.e. produces `*_code_slot_*_pin_used` events). If none of your locks
24	    support this, the counter will never decrement.
25	
26	
27	    **Setup**
28	
29	    1. Create an `input_number` helper (Settings → Devices & Services → Helpers)
30	       with min=-1, max=your desired limit, and step=1.
31	
32	    2. Import this blueprint and configure it with the PIN used event entity,
33	       enabled switch entity, and the helper you just created.
34	  domain: automation
35	  # yamllint disable-line rule:line-length
36	  source_url: https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/blob/main/blueprints/automation/lock_code_manager/slot_usage_limiter.yaml
37	  input:
38	    config_entry:
39	      name: Lock Code Manager config entry
40	      description: >
41	        The Lock Code Manager config entry that manages your lock(s).
42	        Used to identify the setup in notifications.
43	      selector:
44	        config_entry:
45	          integration: lock_code_manager
46	    slot_number:
47	      name: Code slot number
48	      description: The Lock Code Manager code slot number.
49	      selector:
50	        number:
51	          min: 1
52	          max: 999
53	          mode: box
54	    pin_used_entity:
55	      name: PIN used event entity
56	      description: >
57	        The Lock Code Manager event entity that fires when the PIN is used
58	        (e.g. `event.front_door_code_slot_1_pin_used`).
59	      selector:
60	        entity:
61	          domain: event
62	          integration: lock_code_manager
63	    locks:
64	      name: Locks (optional)
65	      description: >
66	        Restrict the limit to a specific lock or set of locks. When empty,
67	        PIN uses on any lock count toward the limit. When one or more locks
68	        are selected, only uses on those locks decrement the counter — uses
69	        on other locks are ignored.
70	      default: []
71	      selector:
72	        entity:
73	          domain: lock
74	          multiple: true
75	    enabled_switch:
76	      name: Slot enabled switch
77	      description: >
78	        The Lock Code Manager switch entity that enables/disables the code
79	        slot (e.g. `switch.front_door_code_slot_1_enabled`).
80	      selector:
81	        entity:
82	          domain: switch
83	          integration: lock_code_manager
84	    uses_counter:
85	      name: Uses counter
86	      description: >
87	        An `input_number` helper that tracks remaining uses. Set to -1 for
88	        unlimited uses. Create one via Settings → Devices & Services → Helpers
89	        (set min to -1).
90	      selector:
91	        entity:
92	          domain: input_number
93	    initial_uses:
94	      name: Initial uses on re-enable
95	      description: >
96	        When greater than 0, the counter is automatically reset to this value
97	        each time the Lock Code Manager slot is re-enabled. Set to 0 to
98	        disable auto-reset. Set to -1 for unlimited uses on re-enable.
99	      default: 0
100	      selector:
101	        number:
102	          min: -1
103	          max: 999
104	          mode: box
105	    notify_target:
106	      name: Notification service (optional)
107	      description: >
108	        A notify entity to send a message to when uses are exhausted and the
109	        slot is disabled. Leave empty to skip notifications.
110	      default: ""
111	      selector:
112	        entity:
113	          domain: notify
114	
115	mode: queued
116	max: 10
117	
118	triggers:
119	  - trigger: state
120	    entity_id: !input pin_used_entity
121	    id: pin_used
122	  - trigger: state
123	    entity_id: !input enabled_switch
124	    id: slot_enabled
125	    to: "on"
126	
127	variables:
128	  initial_uses: !input initial_uses
129	  uses_counter: !input uses_counter
130	  notify_target: !input notify_target
131	  locks: !input locks
132	  _config_entry: !input config_entry
133	  _config_entry_title: >
134	    {{ config_entry_attr(_config_entry, 'title') }}
135	  _slot_number: !input slot_number
136	
137	actions:
138	  - choose:
139	      - conditions:
140	          - condition: trigger
141	            id: pin_used
142	          - condition: template
143	            value_template: >-
144	              {{ locks | count == 0
145	                 or trigger.to_state.attributes.event_type in locks }}
146	        sequence:
147	          - variables:
148	              current: >
149	                {{ states(uses_counter) | int(0) }}
150	          # -1 means unlimited — skip decrement and disable
151	          - if:
152	              - condition: template
153	                value_template: "{{ current == -1 }}"
154	            then:
155	              - stop: "Unlimited uses — no action needed"
156	          - variables:
157	              new_count: >
158	                {{ [current - 1, 0] | max }}
159	          - action: input_number.set_value
160	            target:
161	              entity_id: !input uses_counter
162	            data:
163	              value: "{{ new_count }}"
164	          - if:
165	              - condition: template
166	                value_template: "{{ new_count == 0 }}"
167	            then:
168	              - action: switch.turn_off
169	                target:
170	                  entity_id: !input enabled_switch
171	              - if:
172	                  - condition: template
173	                    value_template: "{{ notify_target != '' }}"
174	                then:
175	                  - action: notify.send_message
176	                    target:
177	                      entity_id: "{{ notify_target }}"
178	                    data:
179	                      title: >
180	                        {{ _config_entry_title }} — Slot {{ _slot_number }}
181	                        Usage Limit Reached
182	                      message: >
183	                        {{ _config_entry_title }} code slot {{ _slot_number }}
184	                        has been disabled after reaching the usage limit.
185	                        Last used on lock
186	                        {{ state_attr(trigger.entity_id, 'event_type')
187	                           | default('unknown') }}
188	                        at {{ now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S') }}.
189	      - conditions:
190	          - condition: trigger
191	            id: slot_enabled
192	          - condition: template
193	            value_template: "{{ initial_uses | int(0) != 0 }}"
194	        sequence:
195	          - action: input_number.set_value
196	            target:
197	              entity_id: !input uses_counter
198	            data:
199	              value: !input initial_uses
200

When empty, behavior is unchanged.\nWhen set, only PIN uses on the selected locks decrement the counter;\nuses on other locks are ignored. The filter is scoped to the pin_used\nchoose-branch so re-enable resets are not affected.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" && git push -u origin feat/slot-limiter-lock-filter && gh pr create --title \"Add optional lock filter to Slot Usage Limiter blueprint\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Proposed change\n\nAdds the same optional `locks` filter pattern from #1152 to the Slot Usage Limiter blueprint. The input is a multi-select lock entity selector that defaults to an empty list.\n\n- When the list is empty, every PIN use counts toward the limit (existing behavior — fully backwards compatible).\n- When one or more locks are selected, only uses on those locks decrement the counter; uses on other locks are ignored.\n\nImplementation notes:\n- The filter is added as an additional `condition: template` inside the `pin_used` choose-branch, gated on `trigger.to_state.attributes.event_type` (the firing lock's entity ID, as carried by LCM event entities). Scoping it inside the branch — rather than at the automation's top level — means the `slot_enabled` re-enable reset is unaffected, since \"re-enable\" isn't a per-lock concept.\n- Wording in the input description makes the quota semantic explicit (\"uses on other locks are ignored\") so users don't misread it as \"skip notification only.\"\n\n## Type of change\n\n- [ ] Dependency upgrade\n- [ ] Bugfix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)\n- [x] New feature (which adds functionality)\n- [ ] Breaking change (fix/feature causing existing functionality to break)\n- [ ] Code quality improvements to existing code or addition of tests\n\n## Additional information\n\n- This PR fixes or closes issue: fixes #\n- This PR is related to issue: #1152\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Commit, push, and open PR for limiter"
}

> DEVELOPER

does copilots review matter?

> AGENT

Honestly, usually not much. Copilot's PR reviews tend to lean toward stylistic nits, plausible-sounding-but-wrong suggestions on templates/DSLs (it doesn't really model HA blueprint semantics), and occasional duplicate flags of things you've already considered. Skim, don't blindly apply.

That said, it does occasionally catch a real thing — typos, an obvious missing-edge-case, a wrong-looking variable name. So a quick read is worth it; a deep response usually isn't.

> AGENT

Two real comments. Triage:

**#1152 — "fixes #" placeholder is unfinished**
Real but trivial. The PR template's `fixes #` line is still empty in the body. Neither PR closes an issue, so it should be removed (or "N/A"). Worth fixing on both PRs.

**#1153 — `trigger.to_state` could be None**
Mostly theoretical, but it has a real consistency angle that makes it worth addressing:
- `trigger.to_state` is only None if the entity is deleted mid-trigger — practically a non-issue.
- BUT: the limiter already uses `state_attr(trigger.entity_id, 'event_type')` for the same lookup on line 186 of the existing file. My new condition uses `trigger.to_state.attributes.event_type`. Switching to `state_attr(...)` matches the existing pattern, removes the `to_state` access entirely, and is more robust at zero cost.
- The notifier (#1152) has the same construct but Copilot didn't flag it — same fix applies for symmetry.

What Copilot missed (more important than what it caught):
- The limiter trigger has no `not_to: [unknown, unavailable]` filter — so the limiter currently decrements on any state change of the event entity, including HA restart transitions. That's a pre-existing bug, out of scope here, but worth tracking.

Recommendation: push a small follow-up commit to each PR — (a) drop the `fixes #` template line, (b) switch the new condition's template to `state_attr(trigger.entity_id, 'event_type')` for consistency.

Want me to do both?

```json
{
  "command": "git add .github/dependabot.yaml && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nRemove matter-python-client and zeroconf from homeassistant group\n\nThese packages are transitively pinned by Home Assistant itself, so\ngrouping them with the `homeassistant` bump causes Dependabot to\npropose conflicting version pins (e.g. PR #1146 pinned\nzeroconf==0.149.13 while homeassistant==2026.5.3 requires\nzeroconf==0.148.0, making the lockfile unresolvable). Splitting them\nout lets each be tracked individually without forcing the group\nupdate to fail dependency resolution.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" && git push -u origin chore/dependabot-trim-homeassistant-group && gh pr create --title \"Remove matter-python-client and zeroconf from Dependabot homeassistant group\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Proposed change\n\nDrops `matter-python-client` and `zeroconf` from the `homeassistant` Dependabot group in `.github/dependabot.yaml`. Both packages are transitively pinned by Home Assistant itself, so grouping them with the `homeassistant` bump regularly produces conflicting version pins.\n\nConcrete example: PR #1146 grouped `homeassistant>=2026.5.3` with `zeroconf==0.149.13`, but `homeassistant==2026.5.3` requires `zeroconf==0.148.0`. The resulting `requirements_dev.txt` was unresolvable, failing pytest on Python 3.14.\n\nAfter this change, Dependabot will:\n- Continue to group `homeassistant`, `pytest`, `pytest-homeassistant-custom-component`, and `zwave-js-server-python` together (they update in lockstep with HA).\n- Track `matter-python-client` and `zeroconf` as individual PRs, where their pins can be evaluated against the active `homeassistant` version on a case-by-case basis.\n\nPR #1146 will need to be closed (or rebased after Dependabot regenerates the group) so the unresolvable `zeroconf==0.149.13` pin is dropped.\n\n## Type of change\n\n- [ ] Dependency upgrade\n- [ ] Bugfix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)\n- [ ] New feature (which adds functionality)\n- [ ] Breaking change (fix/feature causing existing functionality to break)\n- [x] Code quality improvements to existing code or addition of tests\n\n## Additional information\n\n- This PR is related to issue: #1146\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Commit, push, open PR for dependabot config change"
}
```

  "command": "git add .github/dependabot.yaml && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nAlso drop pytest and zwave-js-server-python from homeassistant group\n\nTrims the group further so it only contains the two packages whose\nversions must move in lockstep: homeassistant itself and\npytest-homeassistant-custom-component (whose releases are tagged\nagainst specific HA versions). pytest and zwave-js-server-python\nhave independent release cadences and don't benefit from being\nbatched with HA bumps.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" && git push && gh pr edit 1155 --title \"Trim Dependabot homeassistant group to HA + pytest-homeassistant-custom-component\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Proposed change\n\nTrims the `homeassistant` Dependabot group in `.github/dependabot.yaml` so it only contains the two packages whose versions are tightly coupled and must move in lockstep:\n\n- `homeassistant`\n- `pytest-homeassistant-custom-component`\n\nThe following packages are removed from the group and will be tracked as individual Dependabot PRs:\n\n- `matter-python-client` — transitively pinned by HA, was producing conflicting pins.\n- `zeroconf` — transitively pinned by HA; conflicting pins broke PR #1146.\n- `pytest` — independent release cadence.\n- `zwave-js-server-python` — independent release cadence.\n\n### Concrete motivation\n\nPR #1146 grouped `homeassistant>=2026.5.3` with `zeroconf==0.149.13`, but `homeassistant==2026.5.3` requires `zeroconf==0.148.0`. The resulting `requirements_dev.txt` was unresolvable and pytest failed on Python 3.14. Grouping packages that HA itself pins is the root cause of that class of failure.\n\n### Follow-up\n\nPR #1146 will need to be closed (or Dependabot will regenerate the group without the offending packages on its next run).\n\n## Type of change\n\n- [ ] Dependency upgrade\n- [ ] Bugfix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)\n- [ ] New feature (which adds functionality)\n- [ ] Breaking change (fix/feature causing existing functionality to break)\n- [x] Code quality improvements to existing code or addition of tests\n\n## Additional information\n\n- This PR is related to issue: #1146\nEOF\n)\"",

1	---
2	blueprint:
3	  name: "Lock Code Manager: Slot Usage Notifier"
4	  description: >
5	    Runs one or more actions when a Lock Code Manager code slot PIN
6	    is used on a lock. Use this to send notifications, trigger scripts,
7	    or run any other Home Assistant action.
8	
9	
10	    **How it works**
11	
12	    When a code slot event fires (PIN used on lock), the automation
13	    runs the configured actions. You can use the action editor to
14	    configure any HA action — notifications, scripts, scenes, etc.
15	
16	
17	    **Available template variables**
18	
19	    Your actions can reference these variables in templates:
20	
21	    - `slot_num` — the code slot number that was used
22	
23	    - `slot_name` — the user-configured name of the code slot
24	
25	    - `lock_name` — the friendly name of the lock where the PIN
26	      was used
27	
28	    - `timestamp` — the date and time the event occurred
29	
30	
31	    **Requirements**
32	
33	    This blueprint requires at least one lock that supports code slot
34	    events (i.e. produces `*_code_slot_*_pin_used` events).
35	
36	
37	    **Setup**
38	
39	    1. Import this blueprint and create an automation from it.
40	
41	    2. Select the code slot event entity to monitor.
42	
43	    3. Configure the actions to run when the PIN is used.
44	  domain: automation
45	  # yamllint disable-line rule:line-length

47	  input:

53	      selector:

61	        When empty, the actions run for any lock the event entity

77	      selector:

> AGENT

Clean. `main` is at `5933ed20` (#1159 plus the dependabot homeassistant bump that landed alongside, since the group is now resolvable). Only `main` and harness internals remain locally.

> DEVELOPER

for the slot usage limiter, why can't we derive config entry and slot number from pin_used_entity

> AGENT

You can, with two HA template functions:

- `_slot_number` ← `{{ state_attr(pin_used_entity_id, 'code_slot') }}` — LCM stores the slot number as a `code_slot` attribute on the event entity (the notifier already reads this from `trigger.to_state.attributes.code_slot`).
- `_config_entry` ← `{{ config_entry_id(pin_used_entity_id) }}` — every HA entity carries the ID of its source config entry, and LCM-created event entities are registered to the LCM config entry by construction.

The variable block would look like:

```yaml
variables:
  pin_used_entity_id: !input pin_used_entity
  _slot_number: >
    {{ state_attr(pin_used_entity_id, 'code_slot') }}
  _config_entry: >
    {{ config_entry_id(pin_used_entity_id) }}
  _config_entry_title: >
    {{ config_entry_attr(_config_entry, 'title') }}
```

That deletes two inputs from the form. Trade-offs to weigh:

1. **Breaking change for existing automations** — the input keys disappear, so users who imported the old blueprint need to re-save (or re-import). With only #1153 just-merged this is probably very few people.
2. **Tighter coupling to LCM's attribute schema** — if `code_slot` ever gets renamed inside LCM, the blueprint silently breaks at trigger time instead of at setup time. (The notifier already has this coupling, so it's not adding a new fragility class — just expanding it.)
3. **Slightly less obvious form** — the explicit inputs document the blueprint's intent ("this is for slot N of this config entry"). Derived inputs make the form simpler but more "magical."
4. **Validation moves to runtime** — `slot_number`'s `number` selector currently does range validation at setup; with derivation, a wrong/unloaded entity surfaces as a None at trigger time.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- The same simplification works for the `enabled_switch` input too: it's `switch.{lock}_code_slot_{N}_enabled`, and LCM exposes it on the same config entry / device. You could derive it via the entity registry by config_entry_id + unique_id pattern — though that's more involved than a single template function, so it's a less clean win than slot_number and config_entry.
- HA's `config_entry_id(entity)` and `config_entry_attr(id, 'title')` are a pretty under-known pair — they let blueprints discover their own context entirely from one user-selected entity, which is exactly the pattern that makes #1159's "pick the event entity, infer the rest" UX feasible.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Want me to do it (one PR replacing the two inputs with derivations)?

1	---
2	blueprint:
3	  name: "Lock Code Manager: Slot Usage Limiter"
4	  description: >
5	    Decrements an `input_number` helper each time a Lock Code Manager code
6	    slot PIN is used on the lock. When the counter reaches 0, the Lock Code
7	    Manager slot is automatically disabled. Optionally resets the counter
8	    when the slot is re-enabled in Lock Code Manager. Optionally restricts
9	    the limit to a specific lock or set of locks.
10	
11	
12	    **Special values**
13	
14	    - Set the counter to **-1** for unlimited uses (counter stays at -1,
15	      slot is never disabled).
16	
17	    - Set the counter to **0** to immediately disable the slot on next use.
18	
19	
20	    **Requirements**
21	
22	    This blueprint requires at least one lock that supports code slot events
23	    (i.e. produces `*_code_slot_*_pin_used` events). If none of your locks
24	    support this, the counter will never decrement.
25	
26	
27	    **Setup**
28	
29	    1. Create an `input_number` helper (Settings → Devices & Services → Helpers)
30	       with min=-1, max=your desired limit, and step=1.
31	
32	    2. Import this blueprint and configure it with the PIN used event entity,
33	       enabled switch entity, and the helper you just created.
34	  domain: automation
35	  # yamllint disable-line rule:line-length
36	  source_url: https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/blob/main/blueprints/automation/lock_code_manager/slot_usage_limiter.yaml
37	  input:
38	    config_entry:
39	      name: Lock Code Manager config entry
40	      description: >
41	        The Lock Code Manager config entry that manages your lock(s).
42	        Used to identify the setup in notifications.
43	      selector:
44	        config_entry:
45	          integration: lock_code_manager
46	    slot_number:
47	      name: Code slot number
48	      description: The Lock Code Manager code slot number.
49	      selector:
50	        number:
51	          min: 1
52	          max: 999
53	          mode: box
54	    pin_used_entity:
55	      name: PIN used event entity
56	      description: >
57	        The Lock Code Manager event entity that fires when the PIN is used
58	        (e.g. `event.front_door_code_slot_1_pin_used`).
59	      selector:
60	        entity:
61	          domain: event
62	          integration: lock_code_manager
63	    locks:
64	      name: Locks (optional)
65	      description: >
66	        Restrict the limit to a specific lock or set of locks. When empty,
67	        PIN uses on any lock count toward the limit. When one or more locks
68	        are selected, only uses on those locks decrement the counter — uses
69	        on other locks are ignored.
70	      default: []
71	      selector:
72	        entity:
73	          domain: lock
74	          multiple: true
75	    enabled_switch:
76	      name: Slot enabled switch
77	      description: >
78	        The Lock Code Manager switch entity that enables/disables the code
79	        slot (e.g. `switch.front_door_code_slot_1_enabled`).
80	      selector:
81	        entity:
82	          domain: switch
83	          integration: lock_code_manager
84	    uses_counter:
85	      name: Uses counter
86	      description: >
87	        An `input_number` helper that tracks remaining uses. Set to -1 for
88	        unlimited uses. Create one via Settings → Devices & Services → Helpers
89	        (set min to -1).
90	      selector:
91	        entity:
92	          domain: input_number
93	    initial_uses:
94	      name: Initial uses on re-enable
95	      description: >
96	        When greater than 0, the counter is automatically reset to this value
97	        each time the Lock Code Manager slot is re-enabled. Set to 0 to
98	        disable auto-reset. Set to -1 for unlimited uses on re-enable.
99	      default: 0
100	      selector:
101	        number:
102	          min: -1
103	          max: 999
104	          mode: box
105	    notify_target:
106	      name: Notification service (optional)
107	      description: >
108	        A notify entity to send a message to when uses are exhausted and the
109	        slot is disabled. Leave empty to skip notifications.
110	      default: ""
111	      selector:
112	        entity:
113	          domain: notify
114	
115	mode: queued
116	max: 10
117	
118	triggers:
119	  - trigger: state
120	    entity_id: !input pin_used_entity
121	    id: pin_used
122	  - trigger: state
123	    entity_id: !input enabled_switch
124	    id: slot_enabled
125	    to: "on"
126

129	  uses_counter: !input uses_counter
130	  notify_target: !input notify_target
131	  locks: !input locks

133	  _config_entry_title: >

135	  _slot_number: !input slot_number

145	                 or state_attr(trigger.entity_id, 'event_type') in locks }}
146	        sequence:

149	                {{ states(uses_counter) | int(0) }}
150	          # -1 means unlimited — skip decrement and disable
151	          - if:

153	                value_template: "{{ current == -1 }}"
154	            then:
155	              - stop: "Unlimited uses — no action needed"
156	          - variables:
157	              new_count: >
158	                {{ [current - 1, 0] | max }}
159	          - action: input_number.set_value
160	            target:
161	              entity_id: !input uses_counter
162	            data:
163	              value: "{{ new_count }}"
164	          - if:
165	              - condition: template
166	                value_template: "{{ new_count == 0 }}"
167	            then:
168	              - action: switch.turn_off
169	                target:
170	                  entity_id: !input enabled_switch
171	              - if:
172	                  - condition: template
173	                    value_template: "{{ notify_target != '' }}"
174	                then:
175	                  - action: notify.send_message
176	                    target:
177	                      entity_id: "{{ notify_target }}"
178	                    data:
179	                      title: >
180	                        {{ _config_entry_title }} — Slot {{ _slot_number }}
181	                        Usage Limit Reached
182	                      message: >
183	                        {{ _config_entry_title }} code slot {{ _slot_number }}
184	                        has been disabled after reaching the usage limit.
185	                        Last used on lock
186	                        {{ state_attr(trigger.entity_id, 'event_type')
187	                           | default('unknown') }}
188	                        at {{ now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S') }}.
189	      - conditions:
190	          - condition: trigger

192	          - condition: template

194	        sequence:

198	            data:

1	---
2	blueprint:
3	  name: "Lock Code Manager: Slot Usage Limiter"
4	  description: >
5	    Decrements an `input_number` helper each time a Lock Code Manager code
6	    slot PIN is used on the lock. When the counter reaches 0, the Lock Code
7	    Manager slot is automatically disabled. Optionally resets the counter
8	    when the slot is re-enabled in Lock Code Manager. Optionally restricts
9	    the limit to a specific lock or set of locks.
10	
11	
12	    **Special values**
13	
14	    - Set the counter to **-1** for unlimited uses (counter stays at -1,
15	      slot is never disabled).
16	
17	    - Set the counter to **0** to immediately disable the slot on next use.
18	
19	
20	    **Requirements**
21	
22	    This blueprint requires at least one lock that supports code slot events
23	    (i.e. produces `*_code_slot_*_pin_used` events). If none of your locks
24	    support this, the counter will never decrement.
25	
26	
27	    **Setup**
28	
29	    1. Create an `input_number` helper (Settings → Devices & Services → Helpers)
30	       with min=-1, max=your desired limit, and step=1.
31	
32	    2. Import this blueprint and configure it with the PIN used event entity,
33	       enabled switch entity, and the helper you just created.
34	  domain: automation
35	  # yamllint disable-line rule:line-length
36	  source_url: https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/blob/main/blueprints/automation/lock_code_manager/slot_usage_limiter.yaml
37	  input:
38	    pin_used_entity:
39	      name: PIN used event entity
40	      description: >
41	        The Lock Code Manager event entity that fires when the PIN is used
42	        (e.g. `event.front_door_code_slot_1_pin_used`).
43	      selector:
44	        entity:
45	          domain: event
46	          integration: lock_code_manager
47	    locks:
48	      name: Locks (optional)
49	      description: >
50	        Restrict the limit to a specific lock or set of locks. When empty,
51	        PIN uses on any lock count toward the limit. When one or more locks
52	        are selected, only uses on those locks decrement the counter — uses
53	        on other locks are ignored.
54	      default: []
55	      selector:
56	        entity:
57	          domain: lock
58	          multiple: true
59	    enabled_switch:
60	      name: Slot enabled switch
61	      description: >
62	        The Lock Code Manager switch entity that enables/disables the code
63	        slot (e.g. `switch.front_door_code_slot_1_enabled`).
64	      selector:
65	        entity:
66	          domain: switch
67	          integration: lock_code_manager
68	    uses_counter:
69	      name: Uses counter
70	      description: >
71	        An `input_number` helper that tracks remaining uses. Set to -1 for
72	        unlimited uses. Create one via Settings → Devices & Services → Helpers
73	        (set min to -1).
74	      selector:
75	        entity:
76	          domain: input_number
77	    initial_uses:
78	      name: Initial uses on re-enable
79	      description: >
80	        When greater than 0, the counter is automatically reset to this value
81	        each time the Lock Code Manager slot is re-enabled. Set to 0 to
82	        disable auto-reset. Set to -1 for unlimited uses on re-enable.
83	      default: 0
84	      selector:
85	        number:
86	          min: -1
87	          max: 999
88	          mode: box
89	    notify_target:
90	      name: Notification service (optional)
91	      description: >
92	        A notify entity to send a message to when uses are exhausted and the
93	        slot is disabled. Leave empty to skip notifications.
94	      default: ""
95	      selector:
96	        entity:
97	          domain: notify
98	
99	mode: queued
100	max: 10
101	
102	triggers:
103	  - trigger: state
104	    entity_id: !input pin_used_entity
105	    id: pin_used
106	  - trigger: state
107	    entity_id: !input enabled_switch
108	    id: slot_enabled
109	    to: "on"
110	
111	variables:
112	  initial_uses: !input initial_uses
113	  uses_counter: !input uses_counter
114	  notify_target: !input notify_target
115	  locks: !input locks
116	  pin_used_entity_id: !input pin_used_entity
117	  _config_entry: >
118	    {{ config_entry_id(pin_used_entity_id) }}
119	  _config_entry_title: >
120	    {{ config_entry_attr(_config_entry, 'title') }}
121	  _slot_number: >
122	    {{ state_attr(pin_used_entity_id, 'code_slot') }}
123	
124	actions:
125	  - choose:
126	      - conditions:
127	          - condition: trigger
128	            id: pin_used
129	          - condition: template
130	            value_template: >-
131	              {{ locks | count == 0
132	                 or state_attr(trigger.entity_id, 'event_type') in locks }}
133	        sequence:
134	          - variables:
135	              current: >
136	                {{ states(uses_counter) | int(0) }}
137	          # -1 means unlimited — skip decrement and disable
138	          - if:
139	              - condition: template
140	                value_template: "{{ current == -1 }}"
141	            then:
142	              - stop: "Unlimited uses — no action needed"
143	          - variables:
144	              new_count: >
145	                {{ [current - 1, 0] | max }}
146	          - action: input_number.set_value
147	            target:
148	              entity_id: !input uses_counter
149	            data:
150	              value: "{{ new_count }}"
151	          - if:
152	              - condition: template
153	                value_template: "{{ new_count == 0 }}"
154	            then:
155	              - action: switch.turn_off
156	                target:
157	                  entity_id: !input enabled_switch
158	              - if:
159	                  - condition: template
160	                    value_template: "{{ notify_target != '' }}"
161	                then:
162	                  - action: notify.send_message
163	                    target:
164	                      entity_id: "{{ notify_target }}"
165	                    data:
166	                      title: >
167	                        {{ _config_entry_title }} — Slot {{ _slot_number }}
168	                        Usage Limit Reached
169	                      message: >
170	                        {{ _config_entry_title }} code slot {{ _slot_number }}
171	                        has been disabled after reaching the usage limit.
172	                        Last used on lock
173	                        {{ state_attr(trigger.entity_id, 'event_type')
174	                           | default('unknown') }}
175	                        at {{ now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S') }}.
176	      - conditions:
177	          - condition: trigger
178	            id: slot_enabled
179	          - condition: template
180	            value_template: "{{ initial_uses | int(0) != 0 }}"
181	        sequence:
182	          - action: input_number.set_value
183	            target:
184	              entity_id: !input uses_counter
185	            data:
186	              value: !input initial_uses
187

```json
{
  "command": "git add blueprints/automation/lock_code_manager/slot_usage_limiter.yaml && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nDerive config_entry and slot_number from the PIN used event entity\n\nRemoves the explicit `config_entry` and `slot_number` inputs from the\nSlot Usage Limiter blueprint and derives both from the\n`pin_used_entity` input using `config_entry_id()` and\n`state_attr(..., 'code_slot')`. LCM-created event entities are\nregistered to the LCM config entry by construction, and they carry\nthe slot number as a `code_slot` attribute, so both values are\navailable without asking the user.\n\nThis reduces the blueprint form from three coupled inputs to one.\n\nBREAKING CHANGE: existing automations built from the prior version\nof this blueprint must be re-imported or re-saved — the\n`config_entry` and `slot_number` input keys no longer exist.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" && git push -u origin refactor/limiter-derive-from-event-entity && gh pr create --title \"Derive config_entry and slot_number from PIN used event entity in Slot Usage Limiter\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n# Breaking change\n\nExisting automations built from the prior version of the **Slot Usage Limiter** blueprint must be re-imported or re-saved after this change. The `config_entry` and `slot_number` input keys are removed; their values are now derived from the `pin_used_entity` input.\n\n## Proposed change\n\nRemoves the explicit `config_entry` and `slot_number` inputs from the Slot Usage Limiter blueprint and derives both from the user's `pin_used_entity` selection:\n\n- `_config_entry` ← `config_entry_id(pin_used_entity)` — LCM-created event entities are registered to the LCM config entry by construction, so this returns the correct config entry ID without asking.\n- `_slot_number` ← `state_attr(pin_used_entity, 'code_slot')` — LCM stores the slot number as a `code_slot` attribute on the event entity (the same attribute the Slot Usage Notifier already reads).\n\nThe form now asks for one PIN-used entity instead of three coupled inputs (config entry + slot number + entity), removing a class of \"wrong slot number for the entity you picked\" misconfigurations.\n\n### Trade-offs accepted\n\n- **Tighter coupling to LCM's event-entity attribute schema** — if LCM ever renames the `code_slot` attribute, this blueprint breaks at trigger time. The Slot Usage Notifier already has the same coupling, so this is not a new fragility class.\n- **Validation moves from setup to runtime** — the explicit `number` selector previously enforced `1 ≤ slot ≤ 999` at setup; an invalid/unloaded entity now surfaces only when the trigger fires.\n\n## Type of change\n\n- [ ] Dependency upgrade\n- [ ] Bugfix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)\n- [ ] New feature (which adds functionality)\n- [x] Breaking change (fix/feature causing existing functionality to break)\n- [ ] Code quality improvements to existing code or addition of tests\n\n## Additional information\n\n- This PR is related to issue:\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Commit, push, open PR"
}

27	@contextlib.contextmanager
28	def patch_blueprint(
29	    blueprint_path: str, data_path: str | PathLike[str]
30	) -> Iterator[None]:
31	    """Patch blueprint loading from a different source."""
32	    orig_load = models.DomainBlueprints._load_blueprint
33	
34	    @callback
35	    def mock_load_blueprint(self, path):
36	        if path != blueprint_path:
37	            pytest.fail(f"Unexpected blueprint {path}")
38	            return orig_load(self, path)
39	
40	        return models.Blueprint(
41	            yaml_util.load_yaml(data_path),
42	            expected_domain=self.domain,
43	            path=path,
44	            schema=automation.config.AUTOMATION_BLUEPRINT_SCHEMA,
45	        )
46	
47	    with patch(
48	        "homeassistant.components.blueprint.models.DomainBlueprints._load_blueprint",
49	        mock_load_blueprint,
50	    ):
51	        yield

152	async def test_motion_light(hass: HomeAssistant) -> None:
153	    """Test motion light blueprint."""
154	    hass.states.async_set("binary_sensor.kitchen", "off")
155	
156	    with patch_blueprint(
157	        "motion_light.yaml",
158	        BUILTIN_BLUEPRINT_FOLDER / "motion_light.yaml",
159	    ):
160	        assert await async_setup_component(
161	            hass,
162	            "automation",
163	            {
164	                "automation": {
165	                    "use_blueprint": {
166	                        "path": "motion_light.yaml",
167	                        "input": {
168	                            "light_target": {"entity_id": "light.kitchen"},
169	                            "motion_entity": "binary_sensor.kitchen",
170	                        },
171	                    }
172	                }
173	            },
174	        )
175	
176	    turn_on_calls = async_mock_service(hass, "light", "turn_on")
177	    turn_off_calls = async_mock_service(hass, "light", "turn_off")
178	
179	    # Turn on motion
180	    hass.states.async_set("binary_sensor.kitchen", "on")
181	    # Can't block till done because delay is active
182	    # So wait 10 event loop iterations to process script
183	    for _ in range(10):
184	        await asyncio.sleep(0)
185	
186	    assert len(turn_on_calls) == 1
187	
188	    # Test light doesn't turn off if motion stays
189	    async_fire_time_changed(hass, dt_util.utcnow() + timedelta(seconds=200))
190	
191	    for _ in range(10):
192	        await asyncio.sleep(0)
193	
194	    assert len(turn_off_calls) == 0
195	
196	    # Test light turns off off 120s after last motion
197	    hass.states.async_set("binary_sensor.kitchen", "off")
198	
199	    for _ in range(10):
200	        await asyncio.sleep(0)
201	
202	    async_fire_time_changed(hass, dt_util.utcnow() + timedelta(seconds=120))
203	    await hass.async_block_till_done()
204	
205	    assert len(turn_off_calls) == 1
206	
207	    # Test restarting the script

> AGENT

Two patterns in HA core, both portable to LCM:

**1. Schema validation** (`tests/components/blueprint/test_default_blueprints.py`)
30-line test that walks the blueprint folder and runs each YAML through `models.Blueprint(...)`. Cheap, fast, catches malformed selectors / bad input keys at CI time. Wouldn't have caught Copilot's `to_state` nit (that's runtime), but would catch e.g. unknown selector keys or schema typos.

**2. Behavioral tests** (`tests/components/automation/test_blueprint.py`)
Per-blueprint. Pattern is:
- `patch_blueprint("name.yaml", path_to_yaml)` — intercepts `DomainBlueprints._load_blueprint` so HA loads from the custom_components path.
- `async_setup_component(hass, "automation", {"automation": {"use_blueprint": {"path": ..., "input": {...}}}})` — instantiates the blueprint with concrete inputs.
- Trigger by setting state: `hass.states.async_set(entity_id, new_state, attrs)`.
- Assert on the side effect via `async_mock_service(hass, "notify", "send_message")` or similar.

**Fit for LCM specifically — what we have and what we'd need:**
- `tests/conftest.py` already gives us `MockLCMLock`, `MockLockEntity`, `MockConfigEntry`, the `pytest_homeassistant_custom_component` plugin, and the custom-integrations auto-enable fixture. So setup scaffolding is mostly there.
- For the notifier/limiter blueprints, the fake event entity needs the right attributes (`code_slot`, `code_slot_name`, `event_type`) on its state — `async_set` handles that.
- For PR #1160's derivation to be properly exercised, the event entity must be registered to an LCM config entry in the entity registry, not just exist as a state — so the test would spin up a real LCM setup via the existing fixtures, then assert that the blueprint discovers `_config_entry` / `_slot_number` correctly without those being passed as inputs.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- HA's `patch_blueprint` is the load-time hook — it doesn't require the blueprint to live in HA's bundled-blueprints folder. We point it at `blueprints/automation/lock_code_manager/*.yaml` directly from the repo.
- The schema test is a near-free win — it'd catch a regression like "I renamed a selector key" within seconds, and would have caught the `to_state` form had it been syntactically wrong (it wasn't). The behavioral tests are where real validation happens: PR #1160's derivation, the lock-filter from #1153, the multi-entity trigger from #1159 — all are observable via mock service calls.
- One LCM-specific pitfall: PR #1160 reads `state_attr(pin_used_entity, 'code_slot')` at variable-eval time, but `state_attr` returns the *current* state — if the test fires a single state change, the variable resolves correctly. If we ever wrote the blueprint to read a historical attribute, the test wouldn't catch it.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Recommendation: do this as two PRs.

- **PR A** — schema test (`tests/test_blueprints.py`, ~30 lines, parametrized over `blueprints/automation/lock_code_manager/*.yaml`). Low risk, immediate signal. Fits the project's "tests run quickly" CLAUDE.md guidance trivially.
- **PR B** — behavioral tests, one test class per blueprint, exercising the input matrix that actually matters (lock filter on/off, multi-entity trigger, derived `_slot_number` matching, counter exhaustion, re-enable reset). Worth its own PR because it's where bugs live.

Want me to start with PR A?

"""Test utility functions."""

import re

import pytest

from homeassistant.core import HomeAssistant
from homeassistant.helpers import entity_registry as er
from homeassistant.helpers.issue_registry import async_get as async_get_issue_registry

from custom_components.lock_code_manager.const import DOMAIN
from custom_components.lock_code_manager.util import (
    async_disable_slot,
    mask_pin,
)

INSTANCE_ID = "test-instance-uuid"
LOCK = "lock.front_door"


@pytest.mark.parametrize("pin", ["1234", "5678", "0"])
def test_mask_pin_returns_hash(pin: str):
    """Test mask_pin returns a pin# prefixed 8-char lowercase hex hash."""
    result = mask_pin(pin, LOCK, INSTANCE_ID)
    assert re.fullmatch(r"pin#[0-9a-f]{8}", result), f"Unexpected format: {result}"


def test_mask_pin_deterministic():
    """Test same PIN + lock always produces the same hash."""
    assert mask_pin("1234", LOCK, INSTANCE_ID) == mask_pin("1234", LOCK, INSTANCE_ID)

Each\nblueprint is a separately-parametrized test case so failures point\nat the specific file.\n\nCatches regressions like unknown selector keys, renamed inputs, or\ntop-level structural errors at CI time. Runs in <1s with no hass\nfixture required.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" && git push -u origin test/blueprint-schema-validation && gh pr create --title \"Add schema validation test for shipped blueprints\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Proposed change\n\nAdds `tests/test_blueprints.py`, which discovers every YAML file under `blueprints/automation/lock_code_manager/` and `blueprints/template/lock_code_manager/`, loads each one, and asserts it parses against Home Assistant's `BLUEPRINT_SCHEMA`. Each blueprint is a separately-parametrized test case so a failure points directly at the offending file.\n\nThis is the same pattern Home Assistant core uses in `tests/components/blueprint/test_default_blueprints.py`. It catches a specific regression class — unknown selector keys, renamed inputs, schema-level typos — at CI time, before users see them.\n\nRuntime: ~0.2s for all 8 blueprints. No `hass` fixture required, so it doesn't add to the integration-test wall time.\n\nBehavioral tests (firing triggers, asserting on side effects) are intentionally out of scope here and will follow in a separate PR.\n\n## Type of change\n\n- [ ] Dependency upgrade\n- [ ] Bugfix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)\n- [ ] New feature (which adds functionality)\n- [ ] Breaking change (fix/feature causing existing functionality to break)\n- [x] Code quality improvements to existing code or addition of tests\n\n## Additional information\n\n- This PR is related to issue:\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Commit, push, open PR A"
}

{
  "command": "git add blueprints/automation/lock_code_manager/slot_usage_limiter.yaml && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nStrip trailing newlines and default missing values in derived vars\n\nAddresses two issues raised on PR review:\n\n1. `_config_entry`, `_config_entry_title`, and `_slot_number` were\n   defined with the folded scalar `>`, which preserves a trailing\n   newline. For `_config_entry` that newline would make the value\n   not match any registered config entry ID when passed to\n   `config_entry_attr()`. Switching all three to `>-` strips the\n   trailing newline.\n\n2. `_slot_number` could render as \"None\" if the event entity has no\n   `code_slot` attribute (entity unavailable, etc.). Added\n   `| default('?')` to match the notifier's `slot_num` template.\n   Also added `| default('Lock Code Manager')` on\n   `_config_entry_title` so the notification still reads cleanly if\n   `config_entry_attr` returns None.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" && git push",
  "description": "Commit and push the fix to PR #1160"
}

> AGENT

Two parallel updates landed:

- **PR #1161** (PR A — schema validation) opened. 8 blueprints pass in ~0.2s. Ready for review.
- **PR #1160** got a forward commit fixing both Copilot comments:
  1. `>` → `>-` on `_config_entry` (the load-bearing fix — folded-scalar trailing newline was breaking `config_entry_attr()` lookup), plus `_config_entry_title` and `_slot_number` for consistency.
  2. `| default('?')` on `_slot_number` (matches notifier's `slot_num` pattern), and `| default('Lock Code Manager')` on `_config_entry_title` so notifications stay readable if the lookup falls through.

Both Copilot comments were real — the first one in particular would have caused notifications to render with an empty title in production. PR B (behavioral tests) is queued behind #1160's merge.

57	@dataclass(repr=False, eq=False)
58	class MockLCMLock(BaseLock):
59	    """Mock Lock Code Manager lock instance."""
60	
61	    def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
62	        """Initialize mock lock."""
63	        super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)
64	        self._connected = True
65	        self._hard_refresh_interval: timedelta | None = None
66	        self.codes: dict[int, str] = {1: "1234", 2: "5678"}
67	        self.service_calls: defaultdict[str, list] = defaultdict(list)
68	
69	    @property
70	    def domain(self) -> str:
71	        """Return integration domain."""
72	        return "test"
73	
74	    @property
75	    def hard_refresh_interval(self) -> timedelta | None:
76	        """Return configurable hard refresh interval."""
77	        return self._hard_refresh_interval
78	
79	    def set_connected(self, connected: bool) -> None:
80	        """Set connection state for testing."""
81	        self._connected = connected
82	
83	    async def async_is_integration_connected(self) -> bool:
84	        """Return whether the integration's client/driver/broker is connected."""
85	        return self._connected
86	
87	    async def async_hard_refresh_codes(self) -> dict[int, str | SlotCode]:
88	        """Perform hard refresh of all codes."""
89	        self.service_calls["hard_refresh_codes"].append(())
90	        return await self.async_get_usercodes()
91	
92	    async def async_set_usercode(
93	        self,
94	        code_slot: int,
95	        usercode: str,
96	        name: str | None = None,
97	        source: Literal["sync", "direct"] = "direct",
98	    ) -> bool:
99	        """
100	        Set a usercode on a code slot.
101	
102	        Returns True if the value was changed, False if already set.
103	        """
104	        if self.codes.get(code_slot) == usercode:
105	            return False
106	        self.codes[code_slot] = usercode
107	        self.service_calls["set_usercode"].append((code_slot, usercode, name))
108	        return True
109	
110	    async def async_clear_usercode(self, code_slot: int) -> bool:
111	        """
112	        Clear a usercode on a code slot.
113	
114	        Returns True if the value was changed, False if already cleared.
115	        """
116	        if code_slot not in self.codes:
117	            return False
118	        self.codes.pop(code_slot, None)
119	        self.service_calls["clear_usercode"].append((code_slot,))
120	        return True
121	
122	    async def async_get_usercodes(self) -> dict[int, str | SlotCode]:
123	        """Return dictionary of code slots and usercodes."""
124	        snapshot = self.codes.copy()
125	        self.service_calls["get_usercodes"].append(snapshot)
126	        return snapshot

142	        """Return whether this lock supports push-based updates."""

146	        """Subscribe to push-based value updates."""

175	
176	    def __init__(self, name: str, events: list[CalendarEvent] | None = None) -> None:
177	        """Initialize entity."""
178	        self._attr_name = name.capitalize()
179	        self._events = events or []
180	
181	        self._attr_unique_id = slugify(name)
182	
183	    @property
184	    def event(self) -> CalendarEvent | None:
185	        """Return the next upcoming event."""
186	        return self._events[0] if self._events else None
187	
188	    @callback
189	    def create_event(self, **kwargs) -> CalendarEvent:
190	        """Create a new fake event, used by tests."""
191	        event = CalendarEvent(
192	            start=kwargs["dtstart"], end=kwargs["dtend"], summary=kwargs["summary"]
193	        )
194	        self._events.append(event)
195	        self.async_write_ha_state()
196	        return event
197	
198	    @callback
199	    def delete_event(
200	        self,
201	        uid: str,
202	        recurrence_id: str | None = None,
203	        recurrence_range: str | None = None,
204	    ) -> None:
205	        """Delete an event on the calendar."""
206	        for event in self._events:
207	            if event.uid == uid:
208	                self._events.remove(event)
209	                self.async_write_ha_state()
210	                return
211	
212	    @callback
213	    def get_events(
214	        self,
215	        hass: HomeAssistant,
216	        start_date: datetime,
217	        end_date: datetime,
218	    ) -> list[CalendarEvent]:
219	        """Return calendar events within a datetime range."""
220	        assert start_date < end_date
221	        events = []
222	        for event in self._events:
223	            if event.start_datetime_local >= end_date:
224	                continue
225	            if event.end_datetime_local < start_date:
226	                continue
227	            events.append(event)
228	        return events

201	def get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass: HomeAssistant, entity_id: str):
202	    """
203	    Get the in-sync entity object for a given entity ID.
204	
205	    Returns the entity object from the entity component registry.
206	    """
207	    entity_component = hass.data["entity_components"]["binary_sensor"]
208	    entity_obj = entity_component.get_entity(entity_id)
209	    assert entity_obj is not None
210	    return entity_obj
211	
212	
213	async def async_trigger_sync_tick(
214	    hass: HomeAssistant, entity_id: str, set_dirty: bool = True
215	) -> None:
216	    """
217	    Manually trigger a sync tick for an in-sync entity.
218	
219	    Encapsulates the pattern of forcing an entity into a tickable state and
220	    triggering an immediate tick, useful for testing tick-based sync behavior
221	    without waiting for the natural 5-second tick interval.
222	    """
223	    entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, entity_id)
224	    if set_dirty and entity_obj._sync_manager._state not in (
225	        SyncState.LOADING,
226	        SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC,
227	    ):
228	        entity_obj._sync_manager._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC
229	    await entity_obj._sync_manager._async_tick()
230	    await hass.async_block_till_done()
231	
232	
233	async def async_initial_tick(hass: HomeAssistant, entity_id: str) -> None:
234	    """
235	    Trigger initial tick for entity setup.
236	
237	    During entity setup, the initial tick in async_start may fail if dependent
238	    entities (active, code sensor) are not yet registered. This helper triggers
239	    a tick to complete initial state loading, but only if the entity hasn't
240	    been initialized yet (state is LOADING).
241	    """
242	    entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, entity_id)
243	    if entity_obj._sync_manager._state is SyncState.LOADING:
244	        await entity_obj._sync_manager._async_tick()
245	        await hass.async_block_till_done()
246	
247	
248	async def async_trigger_sync_tick_for_manager(
249	    hass: HomeAssistant, sync_manager, set_dirty: bool = True
250	) -> None:
251	    """
252	    Manually trigger a sync tick for a sync manager object.
253	
254	    Useful when you already have the entity object or need to trigger
255	    ticks on multiple managers in a loop.
256	    """
257	    if set_dirty and sync_manager._state not in (
258	        SyncState.LOADING,
259	        SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC,
260	    ):
261	        sync_manager._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC
262	    await sync_manager._async_tick()
263	    await hass.async_block_till_done()

Real Jinja gotcha — `| default('?')` doesn't replace `None`, only undefined. Bundling the fix with the tests, since the tests would otherwise validate broken fallback behavior.

> TOOL

  "new_string": "  _config_entry_title: >-\n    {{ config_entry_attr(_config_entry, 'title')\n       | default('Lock Code Manager', true) }}\n  _slot_number: >-\n    {{ state_attr(pin_used_entity_id, 'code_slot') | default('?', true) }}"
}

{
  "command": "git add tests/test_blueprint_behavior.py blueprints/automation/lock_code_manager/slot_usage_limiter.yaml && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nAdd behavioral tests for slot usage blueprints\n\nExercises the two LCM blueprints end-to-end against the real YAML\non disk. Each test uses an LCM config entry with mock locks, fires\na PIN-used event via the lock fixture, and asserts on the side\neffects via mocked services.\n\nNotifier coverage: single-entity trigger, multi-entity trigger\n(PR #1159), lock filter allow / block (PR #1152), template\nvariable resolution.\n\nLimiter coverage: counter decrement, switch.turn_off on\nexhaustion, unlimited (-1) skipping decrement, lock filter\ngating decrement (PR #1153), and derivation of `_slot_number` and\n`_config_entry_title` from the PIN-used event entity (PR #1160).\n\nAlso fixes a Jinja gotcha raised in PR #1160 review: `| default()`\nwithout the boolean flag does not replace `None` (only undefined).\nconfig_entry_attr() and state_attr() can return None, so the\nfallback defaults need `, true` to actually substitute on the\nNone case.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" && git push -u origin test/blueprint-behavioral && gh pr create --title \"Add behavioral tests for slot usage blueprints\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Proposed change\n\nAdds end-to-end behavioral tests for the **Slot Usage Notifier** and **Slot Usage Limiter** blueprints, exercising the real YAML files via HA's `patch_blueprint` pattern (same approach used in `homeassistant/tests/components/automation/test_blueprint.py`).\n\nEach test reuses the existing `mock_lock_config_entry` + `lock_code_manager_config_entry` fixtures to spin up a real LCM setup with two mock locks, instantiates the blueprint via `automation.use_blueprint`, fires a PIN-used event with `lock.async_fire_code_slot_event(...)`, and asserts on the side effects through `async_mock_service`.\n\n### Coverage\n\n**Notifier**\n- Single-entity trigger renders `slot_num` / `slot_name` / `lock_name` correctly.\n- Multi-entity trigger fires for any selected entity (PR #1159).\n- Lock filter allows matching firing lock (PR #1152).\n- Lock filter blocks non-matching firing lock (PR #1152).\n\n**Limiter**\n- Counter decrements by 1 on each PIN use.\n- `switch.turn_off` is called when the counter hits 0.\n- Unlimited (`-1`) counter is skipped — no decrement, no disable.\n- Lock filter prevents decrement when the firing lock isn't in the set (PR #1153).\n- Derived `_slot_number` and `_config_entry_title` (PR #1160) resolve to the correct values from the event entity's `code_slot` attribute and `config_entry_id()`.\n\n### Drive-by fix\n\nWhile writing the derivation test I hit the Jinja gotcha raised in the post-merge review of PR #1160: `| default('foo')` does **not** replace `None` (it only replaces undefined). `state_attr()` and `config_entry_attr()` both return `None` when their lookup fails, so the fallback defaults in `slot_usage_limiter.yaml` needed `default('...', true)` to actually substitute on the None case. Without that, the notification title would render as the literal string \"None — Slot None\" if the entity was missing.\n\nTest wall time: ~0.8s for all 9 tests.\n\n## Type of change\n\n- [ ] Dependency upgrade\n- [ ] Bugfix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)\n- [ ] New feature (which adds functionality)\n- [ ] Breaking change (fix/feature causing existing functionality to break)\n- [x] Code quality improvements to existing code or addition of tests\n\n## Additional information\n\n- This PR is related to issue: #1152, #1153, #1159, #1160\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Commit, push, open PR B"
}
```
